Appendix C 


in the making of any payment of principal (and 
premium, if any) or interest or Additional Amounts, 
if any; and 


(3) at any time during the continuance of any 
such default, upon the written request of the 
Trustee, forthwith pay to the Trustee all sums so 
held in trust by such Paying Agent. 


The Company may at any time, for the purpose of 
obtaining the satisfaction and discharge of this Indenture or 
for any other purpose, pay, or by Company Order direct any 
Paying Agent to pay, to the Trustee all sums held in trust by 
the Company or such Paying Agent, such sums to be held by 
the Trustee upon the same trusts as those upon which such 
sums were held by the Company or such Paying Agent; and, 
upon such payment by any Paying Agent to the Trustee, such 
Paying Agent shall be released from all further liability with 
respect to such sums. 


Any money deposited with the Trustee or any Paying 
Agent, or then held by the Company, in trust for the payment 
of the principal of (or premium, if any) or interest or 
Additional Amounts, if any, on any Note and remaining 
unclaimed for two years after such principal, premium or 
interest or Additional Amounts, if any, has become due and 
payable shall be paid to the Company on Company Request, 
or (if then held by the Company) shall be discharged from 
such trust; and the Holder of such Note shall thereafter, as 
an unsecured general creditor, look only to the Company for 
payment thereof, and all liability of the Trustee or such Paying 
Agent with respect to such trust money, and all liability of 


159a 
Appendix C - 


the Company as trustee thereof, shall thereupon cease; 
provided, however, that the Trustee or such Paying Agent, 
before being required to make any such repayment, may at 
the expense of the Company cause to be published once, in a 
newspaper published in the English language, customarily 
published on each Business Day and of general circulation 
in the Borough of Manhattan, The City of New York, notice 
that such money remains unclaimed and that, after a date 
specified therein, which shall not be less than 30 days from 
the date of such publication, any unclaimed balance of such 
money then remaining will be repaid to the Company. 


SECTION 1004. Corporate Existence. 


Subject to Article Eight, the Company will do or cause 
to be done all things necessary to preserve and keep in full 
force and effect its corporate existence and the rights (charter 
and statutory) and franchises of the Company and each 
Subsidiary of the Company; provided, however, that the 
Company shall not be required to preserve any such right or 
franchise if the Board of Directors shall determine that the 
preservation thereof is no longer desirable in the conduct of 
the business of the Company and its Subsidiaries as a whole 
and that the loss thereof is not disadvantageous in any 
material respect to the Holders. 


SECTION 1005. Payment of Taxes. 


The Company shall, and shall cause its Subsidiaries to, 
pay and discharge when due and payable all taxes imposed 
On it or on its income or profits or on any of its properties 
except such taxes as are being contested in good faith in 
appropriate proceedings. 


160a 


Appendix C 
SECTION 1006. Maintenance of Properties. 


The Company will cause all properties owned by the 
Company or any Subsidiary of the Company or used or held 
for use in the conduct of its business or the business of any 
such Subsidiary to be maintained and kept in good condition, 
repair and working order and supplied with all necessary 
equipment and will cause to be made all necessary repairs, 
renewals, replacements, betterments and improvements 
thereof, all as in the judgment of the Company may be 
necessary so that the business carried on in connection 
therewith may be properly and advantageously conducted at 
all times; provided, however, that nothing in this Section shall 
prevent the Company or any Subsidiary of the Company from 
discontinuing the maintenance of any of such properties if 
such discontinuance is, in the judgment of the Company or 
such Subsidiary, desirable in the conduct of the business of 
the Company and its Subsidiaries as a whole and not 
disadvantageous in any material respect to the Holders. 


SECTION 1007. Maintenance of Insurance. 


The Company will cause each of the Project Toll Road 
Joint Ventures to maintain insurance policies covering such 
risks, and in such amounts and with such terms as are 
normally carried by similarly situated foreign invested 
companies engaged in the construction, ownership, operation 
and maintenance of toll roads in the Peoples’ Republic of 
China and to provide the Trustee with evidence of such 
insurance on an annual basis, which evidence shall be in the 
form of an Officer’s Certificate certifying as to the 
compliance with this Section 1007.

SECTION 1008. Statement by Officers As to Default. 


(a) The Company will deliver to the Trustee, within 180 
days after the end of each fiscal year and 90 days after the 
end of each fiscal semiannual period, a brief certificate from 
the principal executive officer, principal financial officer or 
principal accounting officer, as to his or her knowledge of 
the Company’s compliance with all conditions and covenants 
under this Indenture. For purposes of this Section 1008(a), 
such compliance shall be determined without regard to any 
period of grace or requirement of notice under this Indenture. 


(b) When any Default or Event of Default has occurred 
and is continuing under this Indenture, the Company shall 
deliver to the Trustee by registered or certified mail or by 
telegram, telex or facsimile transmission an Officer’s 
Certificate specifying such event, notice or other action 
within five Business Days of its occurrence.

SECTION 1011. Limitation on Restricted Payments. 


(a) The Company will not, and will not permit any 
Restricted Subsidiary to, directly or indirectly, make any 
Restricted Payment unless, at the time of such action and 
after giving effect thereto, (i) no Default or Event of Default 
shall have occurred and be continuing or would result 
therefrom, (ii) the Debt Service Coverage Ratio is not less

(iii) the aggregate amount on deposit in the Interest Payment

interest (including Additional Interest, if any) due and payable 
on the Notes within 365 days following the most recent 
Determination Date, and (iv) there shall be on deposit in the 
Note Redemption Account an amount sufficient to redeem 
the aggregate principal amount of Notes required to be

Determination Date, provided that if the Company has met

this Section 1011, the Company may make such Restricted

The Company will not, and will not permit any Restricted

SECTION 1015. Purchase of Notes upon Change 
in Control. 


(a) Upon the occurrence of a Change of Control, the 
Company shall be obligated to repurchase, at the election of 
each holder of Notes, all or any part of such holder’s Notes 
at a repurchase price equal to 101% of the principal amount 
thereof plus accrued and unpaid interest (including Additional 
Interest, if any), to the date of repurchase (subject to the nght 
of holders of record on the relevant record date to receive 
interest due on the relevant Interest Payment Date) pursuant 
to the offer described below (the “Change of Control Offer”). 


(b) Within 30 days following any Change in Control, 
the Company shall give to each Holder of the Notes in the 
manner provided in Section 106 a notice stating: 


(1) that a Change in Control has occurred 
with respect to the Company and that such Holder 
has the right to require the Company to repurchase 
all or any part of such Holder’s Notes at a 
repurchase price in cash equal to 101% of the 
principal amount thereof plus accrued and unpaid 
interest (including Additional Interest, if any), 


173a 
Appendix C 


to the date of repurchase (subject to the right of 
Holders of record on the relevant record date to 
receive interest due on the relevant interest 
payment date); 


(2) the circumstances and relevant facts 
regarding such Change in Control (including but 
not limited to information with respect to pro 
forma historical income, cash flow and 
capitalization after giving effect to such Change 
in Control, if applicable); 


(3) the repurchase date (the “Repurchase 
Date’’) (which, except as otherwise required by 
applicable law, will be no earlier than 30 days nor 
later than 60 days from the date such notice is 
mailed); and 


(4) the instructions a Holder must follow in 
order to have such Holder’s Notes repurchased in 
accordance with paragraph (c) of this Section. 


(c) Holders electing to have Notes purchased will be 
required to surrender such Notes to the Company at the 
address specified in the notice at least five Business Days 
prior to the Repurchase Date. Holders will be entitled to 
withdraw their election if the Company receives, not later 
than three Business Days prior to the Repurchase Date, a 
telegram, telex, facsimile transmission or letter setting forth 
the name of the Holder, the principal amount of the Notes 
delivered for purchase by the Holder as to which his election 
is to be withdrawn, and a statement that such Holder is 


174a 


Appendix C 


withdrawing his election to have such Notes purchased. 
Holders whose Notes are purchased only in part will be issued 
new Notes equal in principal amount to the unpurchased 
portion of the Notes surrendered. 


(d) The Company will comply with the applicable tender 
offer laws, rules and regulations, including Rule 14e-1 under 
the Exchange Act, and any other applicable securities laws 
and regulations and any applicable requirements of any 
securities exchange on which the Notes are listed in 
connection with a Change of Control Offer. To the extent 
that the provisions of any securities laws or regulations or 
any applicable requirements of any securities exchange on 
which the Notes are listed conflict with the provisions of 
this Indenture, the Company will comply with the applicable 
securities laws and regulations and any applicable 
requirements of any securities exchange on which the Notes 
are listed and shall not be deemed to have breached its 
obligations described in this Indenture by virtue thereof. 


(e) The Company shall not, and shall not permit any 
Restricted Subsidiary to, create or permit to exist or become 
effective any restriction (other than restrictions existing under 
Indebtedness or the Security Documents as in effect on the 
date of this Indenture) that would materially impair the ability 
of the Company to make a Change of Control Offer to 
purchase the Notes or, if such Change of Control Offer is 
made, to pay for the Notes tendered for purchase. 


SECTION 1016. Limitation on Sales of Assets. 


(a) The Company will not, and will not permit any 
Restricted Subsidiary, to make any Asset Disposition, other 


175a 


Appendix C 


than to the Company or a Wholly Owned Subsidiary, unless

assets, property or Capital Stock sold, transferred or otherwise 
disposed of in Asset Dispositions, during the four full fiscal

less than 1.75 to 1.

so long as such sales arise from the upgrade or improvement 
of existing facilities, and (ii) dispose of the Company’s 
indirect interests in the Non-Project Toll Road Joint Ventures 
in accordance with Section 1011(b)(1) of this Indenture.

SECTION 1022. Operation and Maintenance; 
Engineering Reports; Audit of Toll Collection System. 


(a) The Company shall, and shall cause each of the 
Project Toll Road Joint Ventures to operate and maintain each 
Toll Road in accordance with prudent industry operating and 
maintenance standards and practices generally accepted in 
the operation of toll road projects. The Company shall engage 
an independent engineering firm to complete an engineering 
report on the status of the toll roads under construction and 
on the condition of the toll roads operated by the Project 
Toll Road Joint Ventures, with a “bring-down” report 
identifying all material repairs which, in such engineering 
firm’s best judgment, should be made to the Tol! Roads, such 
“bring-down” report to be delivered to the Trustee every four 
years. The Company will cause the Project Toll Road Joint 
Ventures to make any and all repairs to the Toll Roads which 
repairs are identified as necessary in the independent 
engineering firm’s “bring-down” report. 


(b) The Company shall engage an internationally 
recognized auditing firm to audit, in connection with the 
preparation of the Company’s annual audited financial 


179a 


Appendix C 


statements, the toil collections system of each of the Project 
Toll Roads and to deliver such audit to the Trustee. In the 
event that such audit discloses material deficiencies in any 
of the Project Toll Road toll collection systems then the 
Company shall have 60 days to cure such material deficiency 
as evidenced by the delivery of a certification of the auditing 
firm to the Trustee to the effect that such material deficiency 
has been rectified.

ARTICLE ELEVEN 
REDEMPTION OF NOTES 
SECTION 1101. Right of Redemption. 


(a) The Notes may be redeemed at the option of the 
Company, subject to the conditions and at the Redemption 
Prices specified in the form of Note under the caption 
“5. Redemption — Optional Redemption”, together with 
accrued interest (including Additional Interest, if any) to the 
Redemption Date and Additional Amounts, if any. 


(b) The Notes shall be redeemed on the dates and at the 
Redemption Prices specified in the form of Note under the 
caption “5. Redemption — Mandatory Redemption”, together 
with accrued interest (including Additional Interest, if any) 
to the Mandatory Redemption Date and Additional Amounts, 
if any. 


SECTION 1102. Applicability of Article. 


Redemption of Notes at the election of the Company or 
otherwise, as permitted or required by any provision of this 
Indenture, shall be made in accordance with such provision 
and this Article. 


186a 


Appendix C 
SECTION 1103. Election to Redeem; Notice to Trustee. 


The election of the Company to redeem any Notes 
pursuant to Section 1101(a) shall be evidenced by a Board 
Resolution. In case of any redemption at the election of the 
Company, the Company shall, at least 60 days prior to the 
Redemption Date fixed by the Company (unless a shorter 
notice shall be satisfactory to the Trustee), notify the Trustee 
in writing of such Redemption Date and of the principal 
amount of Notes to be redeemed and shall deliver to the 
Trustee such documentation and records as shall enable the 
Trustee to select the Notes to be redeemed pursuant to Section 
1104. 


SECTION 1104. Selection by Trustee of Notes to Be 
Redeemed. 


If less than all the Notes are to be redeemed, the particular 
Notes to be redeemed shall be selected not more than 
60 days prior to the Redemption Date by the Trustee, from 
the Outstanding Notes not previously called for redemption, 
by such method as the Trustee shall deem fair and appropriate 
and which may provide for the selection for redemption of 
portions of the principal of Notes; provided, however, that 
no such partial redemption shall reduce the portion of the 
principal amount of a Note not redeemed to less than $1,000. 


The Trustee shall promptly notify the Company in writing 
of the Notes selected for redemption and, in the case of any 
Notes selected for partial redemption, the principal amount 
thereof to be redeemed. 


187a 


Appendix C 


For all purposes of this Indenture, unless the context 
otherwise requires, all provisions relating to redemption of 
Notes shall relate, in the case of any Note redeemed or to be 
redeemed only in part, to the portion of the principal amount 
of such Note which has been or is to be redeemed. 


SECTION 1105. Notice of Redemption. 


Notice of redemption shall be given in the manner 
provided for in Section 106 not less than 30 nor more than 
60 days prior to the Redemption Date, to each Holder of Notes 
to be redeemed. 


All notices of redemption shall state: 
(1) the Redemption Date, 


(2) the Redemption Price and the amount of accrued 
interest to the Redemption Date payable as provided in 
Section 1107, if any, 


(3) if less than all Outstanding Notes are to be redeemed, 
the identification (and, in the case of a partial redemption, 
the principal amounts) of the particular Notes to be redeemed, 


(4) in case any Note is to be redeemed in part only, the 
notice which relates to such Note shall state that on and after 
the Redemption Date, upon surrender of such Note, the 
Holder will receive, without charge, a new Note or Notes of 
authorized denominations for the principal amount thereof 
remaining unredeemed, 


188a 


Appendix C 


(5) that on the Redemption Date, the Redemption Price 
(and accrued interest, if any, to the Redemption Date payable 
as provided in Section 1107) will become due and payable 
upon each such Note, or the portion thereof, to be redeemed, 
and that interest thereon will cease to accrue on and after 
said date, and 


(6) the place or places where such Notes are to be 
surrendered for payment of the Redemption Price and accrued 
interest, if any. 


Notice of redemption of Notes to be redeemed at the 
election of the Company shall be given by the Company or, 
at the Company’s request, by the Trustee in the name and at 
the expense of the Company. 


SECTION 1106. Deposit of Redemption Price. 


Prior to any Redemption Date, the Company shall deposit 
with the Trustee or with a Paying Agent (or, if the Company 
is acting as its own Paying Agent, segregate and hold in trust 
as provided in Section 1003) an amount of money sufficient 
to pay the Redemption Price of, and any applicable Additional 
Amounts and accrued interest on, all the Notes which are to 
be redeemed on that date. 


SECTION 1107. Notes Payable on Redemption Date. 
a 
Notice of redemption having been given as aforesaid, 
the Notes so to be redeemed shall, on the Redemption Date, 
become due and payable at the Redemption Price therein 
specified (together with accrued interest, if any, to the

Redemption Date and Additional Amounts, if any), and from 
and after such date (unless the Company shall default in the

accordance with said notice, such Note shall be paid by the 
Company at the Redemption Price, together with accrued 
interest, if any, to the Redemption Date and Additional

If any Note called for redemption shall not be so paid 
upon surrender thereof for redemption, the principal (and 
premium, if any) shall, until paid, bear interest from the

ARTICLE TWELVE 
DEFEASANCE AND COVENANT DEFEASANCE 


SECTION 1201. Company's Option to Effect Defeasance 
or Covenant Defeasance. 


The Company may, at its option and, at any time, with 
respect to the Notes, elect to have either Section 1202 or Section 
1203 be applied to all Outstanding Notes upon compliance with 
the conditions set forth below in this Article Twelve. 


190a 


Appendix C 
SECTION 1202. Defeasance and Discharge. 


Upon the Company’s exercise under Section 1201 of the 
option applicable to this Section 1202, the Company shall 
be deemed to have been discharged from its obligations with 
respect to all Outstanding Notes on the date the conditions set 
forth in Section 1204 are satisfied (hereinafter, “defeasance’’). 
For this purpose, such defeasance means that the Company 
shall be deemed to have paid and discharged the entire 
Indebtedness represented by the Outstanding Notes, which shall 
thereafter be deemed to be “Outstanding” only for the purposes 
of Section 1205 and the other Sections of this Indenture referred 
to in (A) and (B) below, and to have satisfied all its other 
obligations under such Notes and this Indenture insofar as such 
Notes are concerned (and the Trustee, at the request and expense 
of the Company, shall execute proper instruments 
acknowledging the same), except for the following which shall 
survive until otherwise terminated or discharged hereunder: 
(A) the rights of Holders of Outstanding Notes to receive, 
solely from the trust fund described in Section 1204 and as 
more fully set forth in such Section, payments in respect of 
the principal of (and premium, if any, on), interest on and 
Additional Amounts, if any, in respect of such Notes when 
such payments are due, (B) the Company’s obligations with 
respect to such Notes under Sections 112, 304, 305, 306, 307, 
308, 515, 608(e), 1002, 1003 and 1009, (C) the rights, powers, 
trusts, duties and immunities of the Trustee hereunder and 
(D) this Article Twelve. Subject to compliance with this 
Article Twelve, the Company may exercise its option under 
this Section 1202 notwithstanding the prior exercise of its 
option under Section 1203 with respect to the Notes. 


19la 
Appendix C 
SECTION 1203. Covenant Defeasance. 


Upon the Company’s exercise under Section 1201 of the 
option applicable to this Section 1203, the Company shall be 
released from its obligations under any covenant contained in 
Section 801(a) and in Sections 1007 through 1024 with respect 
to the outstanding Notes on and after the date the conditions set 
forth below are satisfied (hereinafter, “covenant defeasance”), 
and the Notes shall thereafter be deemed not to be “Outstanding” 
for the purposes of any direction, waiver, consent or declaration 
or Act of Holders (and the consequences of any thereof) 
in connection with such covenants, but shall continue to be 
deemed “Outstanding” for all other purposes hereunder. For this 
purpose, such covenant defeasance means that, with respect to 
the Outstanding Notes, the Company may omit to comply with 
and shall have no liability in respect of any term, condition 
or limitation set forth in any such covenant, whether directly 
or indirectly, by reason of any reference elsewhere herein to 
any such covenant or by reason of any reference in any such 
covenant to any other provision herein or in any other 
document and such omission to comply shall not constitute 
a Default or an Event of Default under Section 501(3) and 
501(4), but, except as specified above, the remainder of this 
Indenture and such Notes shall be unaffected thereby.

SECTION 1206. Reinstatement. 


If the Trustee or any Paying Agent is unable to apply any 
money in accordance with Section 1205 by reason of any 
order or judgment of any court or governmental authority 
enjoining, restraining or otherwise prohibiting such 
application, then the Company’s obligations under this 
Indenture and the Notes shall be revived and reinstated as 


196a 


Appendix C 


though no deposit had occurred pursuant to Section 1202 or 
1203, as the case may be, until such time as the Trustee or 
Paying Agent is permitted to apply all such money in 
accordance with Section 1205; provided, however, that if the 
Company makes any payment of principal of (or premium, 
if any) or interest on any Note following the reinstatement 
of its obligations, the Company shall be subrogated to the 
rights of the Holders of such Notes to receive such payment 
from the money held by the Trustee or Paying Agent. 


ARTICLE THIRTEEN 
SECURITY 
SECTION 1301. Collateral. 


As security for the payment and performance by the 
Company of its obligations under this Indenture and the 
Notes, upon the terms and conditions set forth in this 
Indenture, (A) the Company hereby grants to, and charges 
by way of a first fixed charge in favor of, the Trustee for the 
benefit of the Holders of the Notes a first priority security 
interest in all the Company’s rights, title and interest in and 
to (i) the Debt Service Reserve Account, the Construction 
Funding Account, the Joint Venture Distribution Account 
and the Note Redemption Account and all amounts 
deposited therein at any time (the “Collateral Accounts”); 
and (ii) all Dollar Permitted Investments made with amounts 
on deposit in the Collateral Accounts; (B) the Company will 
grant or cause to be granted, and will charge or cause to be 
charged by way of a first fixed charge, (i) all of the issued 
and outstanding capital stock of the Hong Kong Holding 


197a 


Appendix C 


Companies pursuant to the Mortgage of Shares (ii) all sums 
and other monies from time to time owing from a Hong Kong 
Holding Company to the Company pursuant to the 
Assignment of Receivables, (iii) the rights of the Hong Kong 
Holding Companies to distributable cash flows from the 
Project Toll Road Joint Ventures in accordance with the 
applicable Joint Venture Contracts and the other collateral 
pursuant to and as specified in the Collaterai Assignment 
Agreements; (iv) all of the rights of Drew Services Limited 
under the Management Services Agreerrent with respect to 
fees for management services rendered to the benefit of the 
Project Toll Road Joint Ventures pursuant to the Service Fee 
Pledge Agreement; and (v) all distributions, revenues, 
products, substitutions, benefits, profits and proceeds, in 
whatever form, of any of the foregoing. The Holders of the 
Notes will have a claim on the Collateral in respect of the 
Notes held by them that is prior to the claims of all other 
creditors of the Company. 


SECTION 1302. Recording, Etc. 


The Company will cause, at its own expense, the 
Collateral Accounts and this Indenture and all amendments 
or supplements thereto to be registered, recorded and filed 
or re-recorded, re-filed and renewed in such manner and in 
such place or places, if any, as may be required by law in 
order fully to preserve and protect the security interests 
created under the Collateral Accounts and to effectuate and 
preserve the security therein granted to the Trustee hereunder 
for the benefit of the Holders. 


198a 


Appendix C 


The Company will comply with all applicable provisions 
of the TIA, including, without limitation, Section 314(b) of 
the TIA. 


SECTION 1303: Investment of Funds in Collateral 
Accounts. 


So long as no Event of Default shall have occurred and 
be continuing, all funds in the Collateral Accounts shall be 


invested and reinvested by the Trustee, at the instructions of - 


the Company, in Dollar Permitted Investments. If any Event 
of Default shall have occurred and be continuing, the funds 
in the Collateral Accounts shall be invested and reinvested 
in Dollar Permitted Investments by the Trustee. 


SECTION 1304. Debt Service Reserve Account. 


The Trustee will establish a Debt Service Reserve 
Account for the Notes on or before the Issue Date. On the 
Issue Date, the Company will cause to be deposited with the 
Trustee for deposit into the Debt Service Reserve Account 
an amount in U.S. Dollars equal to the amount of interest 
due and payable on the Notes on the next succeeding Interest 
Payment Date. If on any Interest Payment Date there shall 
not be in the Debt Service Reserve Account an amount equal 
to or greater than the amount of interest (including Additional 
Interest, if any) due and payable on the next succeeding 


Interest Payment Date, the Company shall within 30 days of _ 


such Interest Payment Date deposit into the Debt Service 
Reserve Account an amount which, when added to the amount 
already in the Debt Service Reserve Account, will equal the 
amount of interest (including Additional Interest, if any) due 


199a 


Appendix C 


and payable on the Notes on the next succeeding Interest 
Payment Date. Additional Interest shall be included in 
calculating interest due and payable on the next succeeding 
Interest Payment Date only to the extent that Additional 
Interest has begun to accrue on the Notes. Amounts on deposit 
in the Debt Service Reserve Account in excess of the amount 
of interest due on the next succeeding Interest Payment Date 
shall be transferred to the Joint Venture Distribution Account 
on each Interest Payment Date. 


SECTION 1305. Construction Funding Account. 


The Trustee will establish the Construction Funding 
Account for the Notes on or before the Issue Date. On the 
Issue Date, the Company will cause to be deposited with the 
Trustee for deposit into the Construction Funding Account 
US$105,131,250. 


Amounts on deposit in the Construction Funding 
Account may only be withdrawn upon receipt by the Trustee 
of an Officer’s Certificate specifying the amount of funds to 
be withdrawn and that such funds are required to be, and 
will be, contributed by the Company or its Subsidiary in 
respect of a Project Toll Road Joint Venture. In addition, on 
or after the first Business Day of 2000, the Company may, 
upon written request in the form of an Officer’s Certificate 
certifying that there has not been a 1999 Devaluation, 
withdraw from the Construction Funding Account 50% of 
the Excess Project Construction Funds held in the 
Constructicn Funding Account on the last Business Day of 
1999 and apply such funds at its discretion without regard to 
Section 1011; provided, however, that if the Trustee shall 


200a 


Appendix C 


receive an Officer’s Certificate stating that a 1999 
Devaluation shall have occurred, all of the Excess Project 
Construction Funds shall be transferred into the Joint Venture 
Distribution Account. On or after the first Business Day of 
2001, the Company may, upon written request in the form of 
an Officer’s Certificate certifying that there has not been a 
2000 Devaluation, withdraw from the Construction Funding 
Account all of the remaining Excess Project Construction 
Funds held in the Construction Funding Account and apply 
such funds at its discretion without regard to Section 1011; 
provided, however, that if the Trustee shall receive an 
Officer’s Certificate stating that a 2000 Devaluation shall 
have occurred, all of the Excess Project Construction Funds 
remaining in the Construction Funding Account shall be 
transferred into the Joint Venture Distribution Account. The 
Trustee shall have no duty to determine whether a 1999 
Devaluation or a 2000 Devaluation has occurred but may rely 
conclusively on an Officer’s Certificate as to such matters. 


Interest earned and received by the Trustee on amounts 
on deposit in the Construction Funding Account shall be 
transferred to the Joint Venture Distribution account on each 
Interest Payment Date. 


Notwithstanding the foregoing, no amounts may be 
released from the Construction Funding Account if an Event 
of Default has occurred and is continuing. 

SECTION 1306. Joint Venture Distribution Account. 


The Trustee will establish the Joint Venture Distribution 
Account on or before the Issue Date. Pursuant to the terms 


20la 


Appendix C 


of the Collateral Assignment Agreements, the Project Toll 
Road Joint Ventures will deposit into the Joint Venture 
Distribution Account in U.S. Dollars all amounts in respect of 
distributions payable to the Hong Kong Holding Companies 
pursuant to the terms of the applicable Joint Venture Contracts. 
In addition, Capital Contributions made pursuant to Section 
1017 and received by the Trustee shall be deposited in the Joint 
Venture Distribution Account. The Trustee shall first allocate 
amounts in the Joint Venture Distribution Account to a 
segregated sub-account (the “Interest Payment Account”) 
up to the amount of interest (including Additional Interest, 
if any) due and payable on the Notes within 180 days of the 
later of (i) the Issue Date and (ii) the most recent Interest 
Payment Date. Amounts on deposit in the Joint Venture 
Distribution Account may only be withdrawn (a) by the 
Trustee on each Interest Payment Date in an amount equal to 
the amount of interest (including Additional Interest, if any) 
due and payable on the Notes on that Interest Payment Date, 
(b) by the Trustee on each Mandatory Redemption Date in 
an amount sufficient to redeem the aggregate principal 
amount of Notes required to be redeemed on such Mandatory 
Redemption Date, (c) by the Trustee at the written direction 
of the Company and upon receipt by the Trustee of an 
Officer’s Certificate specifying the amount of funds to be 
withdrawn and that such funds will be applied to meet tax 
obligations of the Company and the Hong Kong Holding 
Companies and (d) by the Trustee at the written direction 
of the Company and upon receipt by the Trustee of an 
Officer’s Certificate specifying the amount of funds to be 
withdrawn and that such funds will be applied in accordance 
with Section 1011.

SECTION 1307. Note Redemption Account. 


The Trustee will establish the Note Redemption Account 
on or before the Issue Date. Funds in the Note Redemption 
Account will be applied on each Interest Payment Date 
commencing May 1, 2001 (each, a “Mandatory Redemption 
Date”) to redeem on each Mandatory Redemption Date 
selected Notes held by Holders. The Company will instruct 
the Trustee to transfer into the Note Redemption Account 
from the Joint Venture Distribution Account sufficient funds 
to redeem selected Notes in accordance with the terms of 
the Notes.

event specified in clauses (i), (ii), (ili) or (iv) above occurs, 
the interest rate will again be increased and thereafter reduced 
pursuant to the foregoing provisions. If the Company issues 
a notice that the Shelf Registration Statement is unusable 
pending the announcement of a material corporate transaction 
or otherwise pursuant to Section 3(k) of the Registration 
Rights Agreement, or such a notice is required under 
applicable securities laws to be issued by the Company, and 
the aggregate number of days in any consecutive 12-month 
period for which all such notices are issued or required to be 
issued exceeds 30 days in the aggregate, then the interest 
rate borne by the Notes wil! be increased by 0.50 of one 
percent per annum following the date that such Shelf 
Registration Statement ceases to be usable beyond the period 
permitted above, which rate shall be increased by an 
additional 0.25 percent per annum for each subsequent 
90 day period that such additional interest continues 
to accrue; provided that the aggregate increase in such 
annual interest rate may in no event exceed 1.00 percent, 
and provided further that, notwithstanding anything to the 
contrary contained herein, if such Shelf Registration 
Statement is unusable for less than 30 days in the aggregate 
in any consecutive twelve-month period, the interest rate

borne by the Notes will not be increased pursuant to this 
sentence. Upon the Company declaring that the Shelf

Penalty Interest. 1f on any Determination Date after the

2. Method of Payment. 


The Company will pay interest (except defaulted interest) 
on the principal amount of the Notes on each May | and 
November | to the persons who are Holders (as reflected in 
the Note Register at the close of business on the April 15 
and October 15 immediately preceding such Interest Payment 
Date), in each case, even if the Note is cancelled on 
registration of transfer or registration of exchange after such 
record date; provided that, with respect to the final payment 
of principal, the Company will make payment to the Holder 
that surrenders this Note to any Paying Agent on or after 
May 1, 2006. 


The Company will pay principal, premium, if any, and 
interest in money of the United States that at the time of 
payment is legal tender for payment of public and private 
debts. However, except in the case of Notes in global form, 
the Company may pay principal, premium, if any, and interest 
by its check payable in such money. The Company may mail 
an interest check to a Holder’s registered address (as reflected 
in the Note Register). If a payment date is a date other than a 
Business Day at a place of payment, payment may be made 


219a 


Appendix C 


at that place on the next succeeding day that is a Business 
Day and no interest shall accrue for the intervening period. 


3. Paying Agent and Note Registrar. 


Initially, the Trustee will act as Transfer Agent, Paying 
Agent and Note Registrar. The Company has also appointed 
Chase Manhattan Bank Luxembourg S.A. as Paying Agent 
in Luxembourg. The Company may change any Paying Agent, 
Transfer Agent or Note Registrar upon written notice thereto. 
The Company, any Subsidiary or any Affiliate of any of them 
may act as Paying Agent, Note Registrar or co-registrar. 


4. Indenture; Limitations. 


The Company issued the Notes under an Indenture dated 
as of May 6, 1998 (the “Indenture”), among the Company 
and The Chase Manhattan Bank, as trustee (the “Trustee’’) 
and Chase Manhattan Bank Luxembourg S.A. as Paying 
Agent in Luxembourg. Capitalized terms herein are used as 
defined in the Indenture unless otherwise indicated. The terms 
of the Notes include those stated in the Indenture and those 
made part of the Indenture by reference to the Trust Indenture 
Act. The Notes are subject to all such terms, and Holders are 
referred to the Indenture and the Trust Indenture Act for a 
statement of all such terms. To the extent permitted by 
applicable law, in the event of any inconsistency between 
the terms of this Note and the terms of the Indenture, the 
terms of the Indenture shall control. 


The Notes are general obigations of the Company. The 
Indenture limits the aggregate principal amount of the Notes 
to US$119,000,000.

5. Redemption. 


Optional Tax Redemption. If as a result of any change 
in or amendment to the laws, treaties, regulations or rulings 
of the British Virgin Islands, the PRC or Hong Kong (or of 
any political subdivision or taxing authority thereof or 
therein), or any change in official position regarding the 
appliction or interpretation of such laws, treaties, regulations 
or ru.ings (including a holding, judgment or order by a court 
of competent jurisdiction), which is proposed or becomes 
effective on or after the Issue Date or which, in the case of 
any change in any such official position, shall have become 
available to the public on or after the Issue Date, in making 
any payment due or to become due under the Notes or the 
Indenture, the Company is or would be required on or prior 
to the next succeeding Interest Payment Date to pay 
Additional Amounts, the Notes may be redeemed at the option 
of the Company, in whole but not in part, upon not less than 
30 nor more than 60 days’ notice given as provided in the 
Indenture, at any time, at a redemption price equal to the 
principal amount thereof, plus accrued and unpaid interest 
to the date fixed for redemption; provided that no such notice 
of redemption shall be given earlier than 90 days prior to the 
earliest date on which the Company would be obligated to 
make such withholding if a payment in respect of the Notes 
were then due. The Company will also pay to holders of the 
Notes on the redemption date any Additional Amounts which 
are payable. 


Prior to the giving of any notice of redemption in 
accordance with the foregoing, the Company shall deliver to 
the Trustee an Officer’s Certificate stating that the Company

will be irrevocable.

6. Repurchase Upon a Change of Control. 


Upon the occurrence of a Change of Control, the 
Company will be required to make an offer to purchase on 
the Change of Control Payment Date all Outstanding Notes 
at a purchase price in cash equal to 101% of the aggregate 
principal amount thereof, plus accrued and unpaid interest 
thereon, if any, to the date of purchase in accordance with 
the Indenture. 


7. Denominations Transfer; Exchange. 


Notes are in registered form without coupons, in 
denominations of $1,000 and any integral multiple thereof. 
A Holder may register the transfer or exchange of Notes in 
accordance with the Indenture. The Note Registrar may 
require a Holder, among other things, to furnish appropriate 
endorsements and transfer documents and to pay any taxes 
and fees required by law or permitted by the Indenture. 


8. Discharge Prior to Redemption or Maturity. 


The Indenture contains provisions for defeasance at any 
time of (a) the entire indebtedness of the Company on this 
Note and (b) certain restrictive covenants and the related 
Defaults and Events of Default, upon compliance by the 
Company with certain conditions set forth therein, which 
provisions apply to this Note. 


9. Amendment; Supplement; Waiver. 


The Indenture permits, with certain exceptions as therein 
provided, the amendment thereof and the modification of 


224a 
Appendix C 


the rights and obligations of the Company and the rights of 
the Holders under the Indenture and the Notes, at any time 
by the Company and the Trustee with the consent of the 
Holders of a specified percentage in aggregate principal 
amount of the Notes at the time Outstanding. The Indenture 
also contains provisions permitting the Holders of specified 
percentages in aggregate principal amount of the Notes at 
the time Outstanding, on behalf of the Holders of all the 
Notes, to waive compliance by the Company with certain 
provisions of the Indenture and the Notes and certain past 
defaults under the Indenture and the Notes, and their 
consequences. Any such consent or waiver by or on behalf 
of the Holder of this Note shall be conclusive and binding 
upon such Holder and upon all future Holders of this Note 
and of any Note issued upon the registration of transfer hereof 
or in exchange herefor or in lieu hereof whether or not 
notation of such consent or waiver is made upon this Note.

11. Successor Persons. 

When a successor person or other entity assumes all the 
obligations of its predecessor under the Notes and the 
Indenture, the predecessor person will be released from those 
obligations.

and is continuing, te Trustee or the Holders of not less than 


25% in principal amount of the Notes then Outstanding may 
declare all the Notes to be immediately due and payable. If a 
bankruptcy or insolvency default with respect to the Company 
or any of its Subsidiaries occurs and is continuing, the Notes 
automatically become immediately due and payable. Holders 
may not enforce the Indenture or the Notes except as provided 
in the Indenture. The Trustee may require indemnity 
satisfactory to it before it enforces the Indenture or the Notes. 
Subject to certain limitations, Holders of at least a majority

AFFIRMATIVE DEFENSE 


53. The Defendant, through its ownership in several 
subsidiaries (the “Subsidiaries”), owns interests in thirteen 
Chinese foreign cooperative joint ventures (the “Joint 
Ventures”). 


54. The Joint Ventures are engaged in the development, 
construction and operation of six toll road projects (the “Toll 
Roads Projects”) in the People’s Republic of China 
(“China”). 


55. The Defendant’s interests in the Subsidiaries are the 
Defendant’s sole assets and sole source of income. 


56. The Joint Ventures are foreign investment enterprises 
under Chinese law, specifically China-Foreign Cooperative 
Joint Ventures (“CJVs’”’). 


260a 


Appendix D 


57. The distribution of funds from CJVs to their foreign 
partners or equity holders is regulated by Chinese law, and 
the conversion of such funds from local currency to foreign 
currency, such as the United States dollar, for remittance 
outside China is subject to foreign exchange regulations. 


58. Under the contracts governing the Joint Ventures, 
the Defendant’s Subsidiaries, as the foreign partners in the 
Joint Ventures, are guaranteed a fixed annual return on their 
investment in each Joint Venture. To the extent the income 
of a Joint Venture is insufficient to fund such guaranteed 
amounts, the shortfall is required to be contributed by the 
Chinese partner. 


59. Prior to September 1998, local foreign exchange 
authorities, which are responsible for approving the 
remittance of distributions of CJVs ( including the Joint 
Ventures), regularly permitted amounts derived from 
guaranteed payments by Chinese partners of the Joint 
Ventures to be converted into foreign currency and remitted 
outside China. 


60. On September 14, 1998, the Chinese State 
Administration of Foreign Exchange (“SAFE”) issued a 
notice that, among other things, provided strict standards for 
attracting foreign capital and measures to rectify and prevent 
the guarantee of the return on investment of foreign parties 
by Chinese joint venture partners. 


61. As a consequence of the change in policy of the 
central Chinese Government regarding the remittance of 
distributions by CJVs and other Chinese-Foreign joint 


26la 


Appendix D 


ventures, local authorities became more restrictive and take 
substantially more time to approve the remittance of foreign 
exchange outside China. Approval for the remittance of 
amounts attributable to guaranteed payments by Chinese joint 
venture partners in particular became especially difficult 
and was granted only after special approval from SAFE had 
been obtained. 


62. The Defendant became incapable of making the 
payments required under the Indenture as a result of its 
Subsidiaries’ inability to collect in a timely fashion the 
guaranteed payments from their Chinese partners due to the 
regulatory and policy changes under Chinese law. 


63. The Defendant’s alleged defaults under the Indenture 
are due to the regulatory and policy changes in China, are 
completely beyond the Defendant’s control and were not 
within its reasonable anticipation. 


64. The Defendant asserts that its alleged defaults under 
the Indenture are excused and discharged pursuant to the 
doctrine of impossibility of performance. 


WHEREFORE, the Defendant respectfully requests that 
the Court dismiss the complaint and grant it such other and 
further relief as is just.

August 6, 1999

Re: The Chase Manhattan Bank v. Traffic Stream (BVI) 
Infrastructure Ltd., No. 00-7721 
Alienage Jurisdiction 


Dear Judges McLaughlin, Sack and Chatigny: 
During oral argument in the above-captioned appeal held 


on Friday, December 1, 2000, the Court asked the parties to 
address the jurisdictional basis for the above-captioned action 


266a 


Appendix F 


in light of Universal Reinsurance Co. v. St. Paul Fire & 
Marine Ins. Co., 224 F.3d 139 (2d Cir. 2000); Koehler vy. 
Bank of Bermuda (New York) Ltd., 209 F.3d 130 (2d Cir.), as 
amended by 229 F.3d 424 (2d Cir.), reh'g in banc denied, 
229 F.3d 187 (2d Cir. 2000), and Matimak Trading Co. v. 
Khalily, 118 F.3d 76 (2d Cir. 1997), cert. denied, 522 U.S. 
1091 (1998), and requested a written response no later than 
Monday, December 11, 2000. 


As the Court noted, the subject matter jurisdictional 
nexus for this action is divined from the alienage jurisdiction 
provision of 28 U.S.C. § 1332. Section 1332 provides that 
when there is a civil action where the matter in controversy 
exceeds $75,000, and where the controversy is between 
“citizens of a State and citizens or subject of a foreign state,” 
federal jurisdiction is present. 28 U.S.C. § 1332(a)(2). 


In Matimak v. Khalily, 118 F.3d 76 (2d Cir. 1997), this 
Court, in a decision that reviewed and considered a myriad 
of precedents concerning this issue, reviewed the district 
court’s decision to dismiss the action for lack of subject 
matter jurisdiction because the plaintiff, a Hong Kong 
corporation, was not a “citizen or subject of a foreign state” 
for purposes of alienage jurisdiction. Because Hong Kong 
— then a British Dependent Territory — had not been granted 
formal recognition by the Executive Branch, the Court 
concluded that it was not a “foreign state.” Jd. at 80-82. 
Nor was a Hong Kong corporation a citizen or subject of the 
United Kingdom. /d. at 85-86. Accordingly, the Court 
concluded that the plaintiff Hong Kong corporation was 
“stateless,” and that alienage jurisdiction was therefore not 
available. Jd. at 86-88. 


267a 


Appendix F 


The holding of Matimak was extended in Koehler v. Bank 
of Bermuda (New York) Lid., 209 F.3d 130 (2d Cir.), as 
amended 229 F.3d 424 (2d Cir.), reh'g in banc denied, 229 
F.3d 187 (2d Cir. 2000), where the Court held that Bermuda 
citizens and Bermuda corporations are not citizens or subjects 
of a foreign state for purposes of alienage jurisdiction. As in 
Matimak, the Court concluded that because Bermuda — 
another British Dependent Territory — has not been 
recognized as a foreign state by the United States, its citizens 
are effectively stateless. Accordingly, because alienage 
jurisdiction was lacking, the Court affirmed dismissal of the 
state law claims against the Bermuda defendants. /d. at 139. 
Similarly, in Universal Reinsurance Co. v. St. Paul Marine 
and Fire Ins. Co., 224 F.3d 139, 140-41 (2d Cir. 2000), the 
Court applied Matimak and Koehler and restated that citizens 
and corporations of Bermuda are not citizen or subjects of a 
foreign state for purposes of alienage jurisdiction. 


Recently, in New Heaven Properties Ltd. v. Grinberg, 
No. 99 Civ. 11546, 2000 U.S. Dist LEXIS 706, *5-*8 
(S.D.N.Y., Jan. 26, 2000) (unpublished memorandum and 
order, a copy of New Heaven is attached), the district court 
addressed the very jurisdictional issue raised by the Court: 
Is an action between a New York corporation and a British 
Virgin Islands corporation an action between a citizen of a 
State and a citizen or subject of a foreign state? Finding that 
there was no distinction in status between Bermuda and the 
British Virgin Islands, i.e., both are British Overseas 
Territories, Judge McKenna, following Matimak, answered 
the question in the negative and dismissed the action for want 
of jurisdiction. /d. 


268a 


Appendix F 


The British Virgin Islands, like Bermuda and the Cayman 
Islands, are considered British Dependent Territories. 
See British Nationality Act of 1981 ch. 61, § 50(1), sch. 6 
(listing the British Dependent Territorfes, which include, 
among others, Bermuda, the British Virgin Islands, and the 
Cayman Islands). Moreover, these dependent territories have 
not been formally recognized as foreign states by the United 
States. See Koehler, 209 F.3d at 139; New Heaven, 2000 
U.S. Dist. LEXIS 706, at *5-*6. Nor are their citizens or 
corporations considered citizens or subjects of the United 
Kingdom. See Matimak, 118 F.3d at 85-86; Koehler, 209 
F.3d at 139 (stating that “a corporation is a citizen or subject 
of the entity under whose sovereignty it is created”). 
Cf. British Nationality Act of 1981 ch. 61, § 50(1) (expressly 
defining the United Kingdom to include only Great Britain, 
Northern Ireland, the Channel Islands, and the Isle of Man). 
Under Matimak and its progeny, it appears that these facts 
warrant dismissal for lack of subject matter jurisdiction. ' 


1. To be sure, Matimak is not without detractors. In Southern 
Cross Overseas Agencies, Inc. v. Wah Kwong Shipping Group, Lid., 
181 F.3d 410, 417-18 (3d Cir. 1999), the Third Circuit expressly 
disagreed with Matimak and instead relied upon the State 
Department's position that a Hong Kong corporation should be treated 
as a “subject” of the United Kingdom for purposes of diversity 
jurisdiction. Of course, the same argument was advanced by the 
Justice Department in Matimak and rejected. See Matimak, 118 F.3d 
at 86. Even on the Second Circuit bench there is some disagreement. 
In an order amending Koehler, Judges Cardamone and Newman 
asserted that absent Matimak, they would rule that citizens of 
Bermuda and other British Dependent territories are essentially 
subjects of the United Kingdom for purposes of alienage jurisdiction. 
See Koehler v. Bank of Bermuda (New York) Lid., 229 F.3d 424 

(Cont'd) 


269a 


Appendix F 


See, e.g., Universal Reinsurance, 224 F.3d at 142 (noting 
the even though action proceeded to final judgment if 
alienage jurisdiction is lacking the decision must be vacated 
and the case dismissed without prejudice). 


In summary, because the British Virgin Islands is not a 
“foreign state,” Traffic Stream, as a British Virgin Islands 
corporation, cannot be a “citizen or subject of a foreign state” 
for purposes of alienage jurisdiction. See 28 U.S.C. § 1332(a)(2). 


Respectfully submitted, 


s/ Shmuel Vasser 
Shmuel Vasser 


Enclosure 


cc: Sarah L. Reid, Esq (by hand) 
Kelley Drye & Warren LLP 
101 Park Avenue 
New York, NY 10178 


(Cont'd) 

(2d Cir. 2000). In addition, Judges Leval, Calabresi, and Sotomayor 
dissented from a denial of rehearing in banc in Koehler and suggested 
that this “issue of exceptional importance” should be reviewed by 
the entire Court. See Koehler v. Bank of Bermuda (New York) Ltd., 
229 F.3d 187, 193-94 (2d Cir. 2000). 


- 270a

ATTACHMENT TO SUPPLEMENTAL LETTER 
BRIEF — MEMORANDUM AND ORDER IN 
NEW HEAVEN PROPERTIES LTD. v. GRINBERG 


NEW HEAVEN PROPERTIES LTD., Plaintiff, -against- 
SERGEY GRINBERG, GREEN SKY INTERNATIONAL 
TRADING CORP., CAPSTONE INVESTMENT CORP., 
JOSEPH F. INGRASSIA, JOHN R. RICE III and 
CAPSTONE & COMPANY, LLC., Defendants. 


99 Civ.11546 (LMM) 


UNITED STATES DISTRICT COURT FOR THE 
SOUTHERN DISTRICT OF NEW YORK 


2000 U.S. Dist. LEXIS 706 
January 24, 2000, Decided 


January 26, 2000, Filed 


MEMORANDUM AND ORDER 


McKENNA, D.J. 
1. 


Defendants Grinberg and Green Sky International Trading 
Corp. (“Green Sky”) move, pursuant to Fed. R. Civ. P. 12(b)(1), 
for an order dismissing this action for lack of subject matter 
jurisdiction. Plaintiff moves, pursuant to id. 41(a)(2), for an 
order of voluntary dismissal without prejudice. 


27la 


Appendix F 


The complaint alleges that plaintiff is a British Virgin 
Islands corporation (but does not allege the location of its 
principal place of business), that the individual defendants 
are residents of New York, and that the other defendants are 
New York entities, the principal “office[s]” of which are 
located in New York. Subject matter jurisdiction is predicated 
on 28 U.S.C. § 1332(a)(2). No federal question is alleged. 


Grinberg and Green Sky assert that there is no subject 
matter jurisdiction because plaintiff’s principal place of 
business is in New York and (in the alternative) that plaintiff 
is not the real party in interest, which is either Boviol 
International Corp. or NASL Corp., both said to be New York 
corporations. 


Plaintiff seeks a voluntary dismissal because it appears 
that there may not be subject matter jurisdiction under 
Matimak Trading Co. v. Khalily, 118 F.3d 76 (2d Cir. 1997). 
Matimak has been followed in several Southern District cases 
cited by plaintiff. Koehler v. Bank of Bermuda (New York) 
Ltd., 1998 U.S. Dist. LEXIS 13574, No. 96 Civ. 7885 (JFK), 
1998 WL 557595 (S.D.N.Y. Sept. 2, 1998); United States 
Fidelity & Guar. Co. v. Braspetro Oil Servs. Co., 1999 U:S. 
Dist. LEXIS 7236, No. 97 Civ. 6124 (JGK), 1999 WL 307666 
(S.D.N.Y. May 17, 1999); and J// Fin. Ltd. v. Aegis Consumer 
Funding Group, Inc., 1999 U.S. Dist. LEXIS 18518, No. 99 
Civ. 2579 (DC), 1999 WL 1080371 (S.D.N.Y. Nov. 30, 1999). 


Defendants Ingrassia, Rice and Capstone & Company, 
LLC, while they join in the motion of Grinberg and Green 
Sky, made it clear at oral argument that their main concern is 
that, if the present case is dismissed and plaintiff brings 


272a 


Appendix F 


substantially the same case in the New York State Supreme 
Court (as it says it intends to do if this case is dismissed) 
plaintiff should not be allowed to then seek dismissal in the 
state court and commence a third action in this Court, with 
the further expense and inconvenience that would cause. 


2. 


The Court considers its subject matter jurisdiction first. 
Filetech S.A. v. France Telecom S.A., 157 F.3d 922, 929-30 
(2d Cir. 1998). 


3. 


The motion of Grinberg and Green Sky presents, as to 
both of its alternative grounds, factual issues which (to the 
extent relevant) cannot be resolved except at a hearing or, 
perhaps, by the submission of further papers, preceded, in 
fairness to both sides, by some discovery. See Kamen v. 
American Tel. & Tel. Co., 791 F.2d 1006, 1011 (2d Cir. 1986). 


The complaint, however, must be dismissed for lack of 
subject matter jurisdiction on a different ground, that brought 
to the Court’s attention by plaintiff's motion. 


28 U.S.C. § 1332(a)(2), on which plaintiff premises 
subject matter jurisdiction, grants the district courts subject 
matter jurisdiction “of all civil actions where the matter in 
controversy exceeds the sum or value of $ 75,000, exclusive 
of interest and costs, and is between ... (2) citizens of 
a State and citizens or subjects of a foreign state.” 28 U.S.C. 
§ 1332(a)(2). In Matimak, the court held that a Hong Kong 


273a 


Appendix F 


corporation was “not a ‘citizen or subject of a foreign state,’ 
under 28 U.S.C. § 1332(a)(2).” 118 F.3d at 88. In Koehler, 
Judge Keenan applied Matimak as controlling in finding that 
two Bermuda corporations (in addition to an individual 
resident of Bermuda) were “not citizens of a foreign state 
for purposes of diversity jurisdiction.” 1998 WL 557595 
at *7. In United States Fidelity & Guaranty, Judge Koeltl 
similarly applied Matimak in finding that a Cayman Islands 
corporation was “not a ‘citizen or subject of a foreign state’ 
within the meaning of 28 U.S.C. § 1332(a)(2).” 1999 WL 
307666 at *4. And in /// Finance, Judge Chin also found, 
under Matimak, that a Cayman Islands corporation was not 
“a ‘citizen or subject’ of a ‘foreign state.’” 1999 WL 1080371 
at *3.' The Court must follow Matimak, and concludes that 
Koehler, United States Fidelity & Guaranty, III Finance and 
Klein all correctly apply Matimak. 


Counsel for plaintiff notes that he is not aware of a 
distinction between Bermuda and the Cayman Islands, on 
the one hand, and the British Virgin Islands, on the other 
(Dannenberg Aff. P 7), and the Court is also unaware of any 
such distinction. //] Finance supports the proposition that 
there is none. See 1999 WL 1080371 at *1 n.1 (classifying, 


1. See also Klein v. Marriott Int’l., Inc., 34 F. Supp. 2d 176 
(S.D.N.Y. 1999), in which Judge Connor applied Matimak to find 
that a Bermuda corporation was “not a ‘citizen or subject of a foreign 
state’ within the meaning of 28 U.S.C. § 1332(a)(3).” 34 F. Supp. 2d 
at 179. Judge Connor noted that, under Matimak, the term “foreign 
state” was to be construed consistently throughout the jurisdictional 
statute, i.e., in the same way in 28 U.S.C. § 1332(a)(2) and id. (a)(3). 
34 F. Supp. 2d at 179 n.10. 


274a 


Appendix F 


as “British Overseas Territories,” Bermuda, the British Virgin 
Islands and the Cayman Islands (among others)).’ 


The location of plaintiff's principal place of business — 
which plaintiff asserts is Russia, although the complaint does 
not allege that — is irrelevant to the subject matter jurisdiction 
issue, because, as put by Judge Bonsal in Mazzella v. 
Panoceanica A/S Panama, 232 F. Supp. 29 (S.D.N.Y. 1964), 
“subsection (c) of 28 U.S.C. § 1332, which makes a corporation 
a citizen for diversity purposes of the state in which the 
corporation has its principal place of business, does not apply 
to alien corporations.” 232 F. Supp. at 31 n.1 (citing Eisenberg 
v. Commercial Union Assurance Co., 189 F. Supp. 500 (S.D.N.Y. 
1960)). In Eisenberg, Judge Dimock explained: 


It is to be noted that the statute differentiates 
between States of the United States and foreign states 
by the use of a capital S for the word 
when applied to a State of the United States. - 
Subdivision (c), therefore, in dealing with the place 
of incorporation refers only to a corporation 
incorporated in a State of the United States. When 
subdivision (c) goes on to deal with principal place 
of business it refers to the same corporation and thus 
only to a corporation incorporated in a State of the 
United States. 


189 F. Supp. at 502. 


2. The source of the cited note in /// Finance is a White Paper, 
“Partnership for Progress and Prosperity, Britain and the Overseas 
Territories,” prepared by the Secretary of State for Foreign and 
Commonwealth Affairs by Command of Her Majesty in March 1999. 
(Ex. A. to Aff. of James Arden, Esq., filed in /// Finance). 


275a 


Appendix F 


In Clarkson Co. v. Shaheen, 544 F.2d 624 (2d Cir. 1976), 
the Second Circuit, in what appears to be dictum, expressed the 
view that the quoted reasoning of Eisenberg is probably the 
correct one. “As alien corporations organized under the laws of 
another country, PRC and NRC are probably deemed to 
be citizens exclusively of Canada for diversity purposes.” 
544 F.2d at 628 n.5. The Court of Appeals described Eisenberg 
as “the leading case on the subject.” /d. 


For the foregoing reasons, the Court concludes that it does 
not have subject matter jurisdiction of this case. 


4. 


In view of the above, the Court need not reach plaintiff's 
motion for voluntary dismissal without prejudice. Aware, 
however, that there are different views of the applicability of 
28 U.S.C. § 1332(c) to alien corporations, see, e.g., Bergen 
Shipping Co. v. Japan Marine Servs., Lid., 386 F. Supp. 430, 
432-33 (S.D.N.Y 1974), the Court notes that, were it to reach 
plaintiff's motion, it would grant it at this early stage of the 
litigation, but on the condition, see Fed. R. Civ. P. 41(a)(2), that 
the dismissal be with prejudice to reinstituting the case in 
this Court on an assertion of subject matter junsdiction based 
on 28 U.S.C. § 1332, in order to avoid the kind of prejudice 
suggested by defendants Ingrassia, Rice and Capstone 
& Company, LLC. 


** * Complaint dismissed for lack of subject matter 
jurisdiction. 


276a 
Appendix F 
SO ORDERED. 
Dated: January 24, 2000 
Lawrence M. McKenna 


U.S.D.J.

278a 


Appendix G 
PRELIMINARY STATEMENT 


Plaintiff-Appellee, The Chase Manhattan Bank, as Indenture 
Trustee (the “Trustee” or “Chase’’), under the Indenture, dated 
as of May 6, 1998 (the “Indenture’”’), of Traffic Stream (BVI) 
Infrastructure Limited (“Traffic Stream”), pursuant to which 
were issued $119,000,000 aggregate principal amount of Traffic 
Stream’s 144% Senior Secured Notes due 2006 (the “Notes’’), 
submits this supplemental brief in opposition to Traffic Stream’s 
appeal from the judgment entered by the United States District 
Court for the Southern District of New York on May 9, 2000, 
based upon the Opinion and Order of the Honorable Shira A. 
Scheindlin, dated January 31, 2000, granting Chase’s motion 
for summary judgment. 


FURTHER ISSUES PRESENTED ON APPEAL 


1. Do the Federal Courts have subject matter jurisdiction 
over an action by a New York corporation against a 
British Virgin Islands corporation under the alienage 
jurisdiction provisions of 28 U.S.C. § 1332(a)(2) in view 
of the uniform positions of the United States, the United 
Kingdom and the Bnitish Virgin Islands that residents 
and corporations from United Kingdom Overseas 
Territories, including the British Virgin Islands, are 
subjects of the United Kingdom and thus entitled to 
invoke such jurisdiction? 


2. Should this Panel overrule Peregrine Myanmar Ltd. v. 
Segal, 89 F.3d 41 (2d Cir. 1996), which held that 
diversity jurisdiction does exist in the case of a British 
Virgin Islands corporation? 


279a 


Appendix G 
FURTHER STATEMENT OF THE CASE 


At oral argument for this appeal on December 1, 2000, 
this Panel, sua sponte, raised the issue of whether subject 
matter jurisdiction existed under 28 U.S.C. § 1332(a)(2) in 
light of the fact that the defendant Traffic Stream was 
incorporated in an United Kingdom Overseas Territory, 
namely the British Virgin Islands. The Panel referred the 
parties to three cases in which the Second Circuit found that 
residents and corporations from Hong Kong and Bermuda 
were not “citizens or subjects of a foreign state” for purposes 
of alienage jurisdiction. Specifically, these cases were: 
Matimak Trading Co. v. Khalily, 118 F.3d 76 (2d Cir. 1997), 
cert. denied, 522 U.S. 1091 (1998); Koehler v. Bank of 
Bermuda (New York) Ltd., 209 F.3d 130 (2d Cir.), amended 
by 229 F.3d 424 (2d Cir.), reh'g en banc denied by 229 F.3d 
187 (2d Cir. 2000); and, Universal Reinsurance Co., Ltd. v. 
St. Paul Fire & Marine Insurance Co., 224 F.3d 139 (2d Cir. 
2000) (collectively the “Matimak line of cases’’). 


This issue is being raised after the District Court ordered 
Chase to foreclose on over $47 million of collateral prior to 
submitting its request for a deficiency judgment. (JA 375). 
See Chase’s Brief as Appellee, dated September 22, 2000 
(hereafter “Chase Brief’), at 6 n.4. Because Traffic Stream 
has not posted any appeal bond or otherwise sought any stay 
pending appeal, Chase, after specific direction from the 
Holders of the Notes (“Noteholders”), disbursed most of the 
$47 million to the Noteholders. Even after this foreclosure, 
Traffic Stream still owes Chase (and the Noteholders) over 
$98 million, which is the amount of the deficiency judgment 
which was entered by the Clerk after the District Court’s 
opinion and order (JA 429-30). See Chase’s Brief, at 14. 


280a 


Appendix G 


Chase respectfully submits that it would be inappropriate 
for this Panel to extend the Matimak line of cases to the 
British Virgin Islands, in view of the 1996 precedent of this 
Court which found that jurisdiction existed in a case 
involving a British Virgin Islands corporation. Peregrine 
Myanmar Lid. v. Sepal, 89 F.3d 41, 45 n.4 (2d Cir. 1996). 
This is particularly true where the public positions of the 
United Kingdom, the Attorney General of the British Virgin 
Islands and the Department of State, as expressed in publicly- 
filed amicus briefs filed earlier this year, are available to this 
Panel and compel the conclusion even under the Matimak 
analysis that the residents and corporations of the British 
Virgin Islands are “subjects” of the United Kingdom, and 
hence entitled to invoke alienage jurisdiction. 


ARGUMENT 
I. 


APPLYING MATIMAK TO THE RESIDENTS AND 
CORPORATIONS OF THE BRITISH VIRGIN 
ISLANDS WOULD OVERRULE THIS 
COURT’S PRIOR PRECEDENT 


In a case that preceded Matimak, the Second Circuit in 
Peregrine Myanmar Ltd. v. Segal was faced with a situation 
where a plaintiff, which was incorporated in the British Virgin 
Islands, brought an action against a defendant that was 
domiciled in New York. 89 F.3d at 45 n.4. The plaintiffs 
asserted state law claims that included breach of contract, 
breach of fiduciary duty, unfair competition and tortious 


28la 
Appendix G 


interference with prospective economic advantage. /d. at 43. 
The Court held that: 


[Defendant] admits on appeal that she is domiciled 
in New York (which the evidence supports) and 
that the district cour! therefore had subject matter 
jurisdiction over the case. [One plaintiff] is 
incorporated in the British Virgin islands and [the 
other plaintiff] is incorporated in Myanmar. Both 
have their principal place of business in Myanmar. 
There is therefore complete diversity between the 
parties. 


Peregrine Myanmar Ltd., 89 F.3d at 45 n.4. 


For this Panel to now hold that there is no subject matter 
jurisdiction because Traffic Stream is incorporated in the 
British Virgin Islands, this Panel would have to overrule 
Peregrine Myanmar Ltd. Rather than overrule Peregrine 
Myanmar Lid., Chase urges this Panel to conclude that there 
is subject matter jurisdiction for actions involving residents 
and corporations from the British Virgin Islands.' Otherwise 


1. Although in New Heaven Properties Lid. v. Grinberg, 2000 
WL 64842 (S.D.N_Y. Jan. 26, 2000) Judge McKenna found that there 
was no subject matter jurisdiction for a case involving a British Virgin 
Islands plaintiff, Chase urges this Court to follow the multiple actions 
from the Southern District that involved parties from the British Virgin 
Islands. See, e.g., One Hundred Pearl Ltd. v. Vantage Sec. Inc., 887 
F. Supp. 636, 637 (S.D.N.Y. 1995); Charles Ulmer, Inc., Sailmakers 
v. Van Ost, 1991 WL 29277 (S.D.N_Y. Feb. 26, 1991) (Defendant in 
caption listed as “BVI” corporation); Lama Holding Co. v. Shearman 
& Sterling, 758 F. Supp. 159, 160 (S.D.N.Y. 1991). 


282a 


Appendix G 


Chase respectfully suggests that the Panel refer the matter 
for en banc consideration in view of the public importance 
of the issues raised, the disagreement among the Court as to 
the application of Matimak (see, Koehler v. Bank of Bermuda 
(New York) Ltd., 229 F.3d. 187 (2d Cir. 2000) (Sotomayor, 
J., dissenting)), and the fact that both the Department of 
State and the United Kingdom have expressed a desire to 
present their views to the Second Circuit in view of their 
disagreement with the Matimak decision. 


MATIMAK WAS DECIDED WITHOUT THE BENEFIT 
OF CERTAIN INFORMATION WHICH WOULD LEAD 
TO A CONCLUSION THAT THE RESIDENTS AND 
CORPORATIONS OF THE BRITISH VIRGIN ISLANDS 
ARE THE SUBJECTS OF THE UNITED KINGDOM 


In Matimak Trading Co. v. Khalily, 118 F.3d 76 (2d Cir. 
1997), cert. denied, 522 U.S. 1091 (1998) the Court held 
that residents and corporations from Hong Kong were not 
“citizens or subjects of a foreign state” for purposes of 
alienage jurisdiction. While the Matimak decision concerned 
Hong Kong, later decisions have applied its analysis to other 
United Kingdom Overseas Territories. See, e.g., Koehler v. 
Bank of Bermuda (New York) Ltd., 209 F.3d 130 (2d Cir.), 
amended by 229 F.3d 424 (2d Cir.), reh'g en banc denied by 
229 F.3d 187 (2d Cir. 2000) (Bermuda); /// Finance Lid. v. 
Aegis Consumer Funding Group, Inc., 1999 WL 1080371 
(S.D.N.Y. Nov. 30, 1999) (Cayman Islands). Before this Court 
extends Matimak to the British Virgin Islands, it is important 
that the Court consider information that is available to this 


283a 


Appendix G 


Panel, which was not available to the Matimak Court, namely 
the amici briefs filed by the United Kingdom and the 
Department of State earlier this year in a case involving the 
very issue of alienage jurisdiction over a British Virgin 
Islands corporation. These briefs, which were submitted to 
the United States Court of Appeals for the Firth Circuit in 
the matter captioned ART 57 Properties, Inc. v. 57 BB 
Property, L.L.C., No. 99-10385 (Sth Cir.) (affirmance 
reported at 212 F.3d 596 (Sth Cir. 2000)), are attached hereto 
at Appendix I.’ 


In particular, the Matimak court did not have the position 
of the United Kingdom before it. Matimak Trading Co., 118 
F.3d at 78 (listing only the United States Department of 
Justice as amicus curiae); see also Favour Mind Ltd. v. Pacific 
Shores, Inc., 1999 WL 1115217, at *4(S.D.N_Y. Dec. 7, 1999) 
(“[In Matimak]} the Second Circuit had before it no 
affirmative evidence that the government of Great Britain 
considered Hong Kong Corporations to be British Citizens.”’) 
Accordingly, as set forth in the UK ART 57 Brief at point III, 
the Matimak Court misinterpreted the law of the United 
Kingdom, and thus altered the result of the analysis that was 
applied in Matimak. 


At the core of the Matimak analysis was “the truism that 
a foreign state is entitled to define who are its citizens or 
subjects.” Matimak Trading Co., 118 F.3d at 85. The Court 
in Matimak then went on to try to determine whether the 
United Kingdom considered corporations from the United

Kingdom Overseas Territories as citizens or subjects of the 
United Kingdom. /d. at 85-86. The Court looked to both the 
British Nationality Act 1981 and the British Companies Act 
1948. Jd. The Matimak court concluded that under the law 
of the United Kingdom, corporations from the Overseas 
Territories were not citizens or subjects of the United 
Kingdom, and thus were “stateless”. /d. at 86. The United 
Kingdom, interpreting its laws, disagrees. 


A. The United Kingdom Considers British Virgin Island 
Corporations to be “Citizens or Subjects” of the 


United Kingdom 


Since Matimak the United Kingdom has made several 
court submissions that make it extremely clear: The United 
Kingdom, under its own laws, considers the residents and 
corporations of its Overseas Territories to be “citizens or 
subjects” of the United Kingdom. See UK ART 57 Brief.’ 


The UK ART 57 Brief states that “[t}he position of the 
United Kingdom Government is that entities incorporated 
in any territory for which the United Kingdom is 


3. The UK ART 57 Brief is by no means the only time that the 
United Kingdom has asserted that the residents and corporations of 
its Overseas Territories are its subjects. This position was also asserted 
in the United Kingdom’s amicus curiae brief in support of a petition 
for a writ of certiorari to the Supreme Court in Matimak (hereinafter 
“UK Matimak Brief”) and the United Kingdom's amicus curiae brief 
in support of the plainuff’s opposition to a motion to dismiss in the

at 4-5. 


The UK ART 57 Brief goes on to explain that while 
“Great Britain” is the collective term for England and Wales, 
and Scotland, the “United Kingdom,” as a State and subject 
of international law, includes Northern Ireland, the United 
Kingdom Overseas Territories and the Crown Dependencies. 
See UK ART 57 Brief, at 5. Like England and Wales, 
Scotland, and Northern Ireland, each Overseas Territory 
and Crown Dependency are separate law districts, entitled 
to enact their own laws in accordance with their constitutions. 
See UK ART 57 Brief, at 6. Although each law district has 
separate laws, they all belong to one state, the United 
Kingdom, and are subject to United Kingdom sovereignty. 
See UK ART 57 Brief, at 6. 


In the UK ART 57 Brief, the United Kingdom provided 
proof of its open declaration of willingness to recognize and 
take responsibility for entities organized under the laws of 
the United Kingdom’s Overseas Territories. See UK ART 57 
Brief, at 6-8. Importantly, under the United Kingdom 
Government’s Diplomatic Service Procedure Manual, Vol. 
7, Annex | (Mar. 1996) (a copy of which is annexed to the 
UK ART 57 Brief as Exhibit 2), the term “United Kingdom 
National” is defined to include “[c]ompanies incorporated 
under the law of the United Kingdom or of any territory for 
which the United Kingdom is internationally responsible.” 
See UK ART 57 Brief, at Exhibit 2, Rule 1(b). 


286a 


Appendix G 


The fact that the United Kingdom would opine that the 
citizens and corporations formed under the laws of the United 
Kingdom’s Overseas Territories are United Kingdom subjects 
is not surprising in light of the “close and wide-ranging” 
constitutional relationship between the “metropolitan” United 
Kingdom‘ and the United Kingdom Overseas Territories. 
See UK ART 57 Brief, at 14. For example, the Governor of 
the British Virgin Islands, which is appointed by the Queen, 
is responsible for core government functions like internal 
security, public service and court administration. /d. 
Similarly, any bill passed by the Legislative Council of the 
British Virgin Islands must be approved by the Governor and 
the Queen, with such procedure including a careful review 
by the Foreign and Commonwealth Office. See UK ART 57 
Brief, at 14-15. 


In fact, the very law under which Traffic Stream is 
incorporated in the British Virgin Islands as an “international 
business company”, namely Chapter 291 of the Revised Laws 
of the Virgin Islands, was enacted pursuant to a specific grant 
in the 1976 British Virgin Islands Constitution.’ This “trend 
sett{ing]” statute has made the British Virgin Islands a well 
known tax haven and has led to it “being characterized as an 


4. The “metropolitan” United Kingdom includes England and
